We took one of those airboat tours, you know, with the huge fan on the back? It was incredibly loud. They give you these cheap little foam earplugs, but they don't do much. We saw a few alligators, mostly just floating there like logs. You'd think they'd be more active, but they're reptiles, so they just bask in the sun to regulate their body temperature. \n\nI used to have a pet iguana when I was in college. His name was Godzilla. Creative, I know. I had to set up this whole heat lamp situation in my dorm room. The RA tried to write me up for it, said it was a fire hazard, but I argued that it was an essential piece of scientific equipment for my biology elective. I wasn't even taking biology, but he didn't know that. \n\nBut anyway, what were we talking about? Oh, right, people plotting against me. No, no, definitely not. It's strictly a problem with management at work. My personal life is perfectly normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed that people were trying to harm you or conspire against you in situations that didn’t involve work?\n\nPatient: Doc, are you testing my memory or something? I feel like we just went over this. Like I literally just said, outside of this issue with the ticket rotation and the audits, everything is completely fine. I still keep my house in order, I pay the bills, I help my wife out. I even handle all the technical parts of my job perfectly—nobody is out there saying my work is sloppy or disorganized. \n\nI actually still pay all our bills by mail, by the way. I know most people do it online now, especially guys in IT, but I like having the paper trail. Though, I will say, the price of stamps lately is getting completely out of hand. Have you seen it? They just raised it again. I remember when a stamp was just a few cents. \n\nMy grandfather actually used to collect them. He had these big, heavy leather binders that smelled like old paper and dust. He'd sit at the kitchen table for hours with these tiny metal tweezers, carefully sliding them behind the little plastic sleeves. He was always hoping to find an Inverted Jenny—that's a famous stamp where they printed the airplane upside down by mistake. It's worth an absolute fortune. He never found one, of course. Mostly he just had regular stamps with birds and old presidents on them. \n\nHe was a very patient man, my grandfather. You need a lot of patience for a hobby like that. I don't think I have the eyes for it anymore, honestly. My prescription changed last year, and I finally had to get bifocals. It took me a solid month to stop tripping over the porch stairs because the bottom part of the lens distorts the floor if you look down too fast. \n\nBut anyway, what I'm saying is, no. No conspiracies or people trying to harm me outside of work. My life outside the office is totally normal.\n\nDoctor: Outside of your concerns about work, have you ever had any unusual beliefs that others don’t share, such as thinking that messages were being sent to you through the TV, radio, or other means?\n\nPatient: Messages through the TV? No, no, I don't think so. I mean, I watch the news, but it's just the news. Mostly I watch those home renovation shows, to be honest. My wife loves them. We were just watching one the other night where this couple bought a house that was completely infested with termites. I mean, the structural damage was incredible. They had to tear out all the load-bearing walls and put in these massive steel I-beams. \n\nI don't know how they afford it, honestly. Those shows always have these ridiculous budgets. The husband will be, like, a part-time butterfly catcher and the wife is a freelance ribbon-tier, and their budget is somehow two million dollars. It makes no sense. But I guess that's television for you. It's all smoke and mirrors. \n\nSpeaking of mirrors, we actually just replaced the one in our master bathroom. The old one was starting to get that black stuff around the edges? Desilvering, they call it. It happens when moisture gets behind the glass and reacts with the silver backing. I had to learn how to hang the new one myself. It's trickier than it looks because you have to use a French cleat system so it sits flush against the drywall. If you just use regular picture wire, it tilts forward and distorts the reflection. \n\nBut, uh, messages? Like secret codes or something? No, nothing like that. Just regular TV.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed that something was happening to you that others didn’t seem to notice or understand, even if it wasn’t related to work?\n\nPatient: Something happening to me that others don't notice? No, I don't think so. I mean, my wife sometimes tells me I don't notice things she does, like when she gets a haircut or moves a piece of furniture. Last week she completely rearranged the living room, moved the sofa to the opposite wall, and I walked right in and sat down to read the paper without even realizing it. She was pretty annoyed about that. \n\nI was reading about this new hybrid car they're coming out with, actually. It's supposed to get something like eighty miles to the gallon. I don't know how they manage that, probably some new battery technology. I've been thinking about trading in my old sedan. It's got over a hundred thousand miles on it, and the transmission is starting to slip a little when it shifts from second to third gear. You can feel this slight hesitation, almost like a little jerk. \n\nI took it to the mechanic, this guy Mike down at the corner garage. He's been working on my cars for fifteen years. He said it's probably the solenoid, but to replace it they'd have to drop the whole transmission pan, and at that point, you might as well just rebuild the whole thing. It's a huge expense. I'm not sure it's worth putting that kind of money into a car that old, you know? \n\nBut anyway, what was the question? Something happening to me? No, no, nothing like that. Just the usual day-to-day stuff. Like I said, the only real issue I'm dealing with is the situation at work. Outside of that, everything is perfectly normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts or beliefs that you know are completely true, but you also realize most other people wouldn’t agree with you about them?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, that's exactly why I'm sitting here talking to you right now. He says baking is completely different up there. You have to adjust the flour and the leavening agents because the air pressure is lower, so things rise faster and then collapse. He tried to make a pound cake for his housewarming party, and it came out looking like a hockey puck. \n\nMy wife makes a fantastic pound cake. She usually bakes one around the holidays and gives half of it to the neighbors. Our neighbors are nice enough, I suppose. They have a golden retriever that barks at the mailman every single day at exactly 11:30. You could set your watch by it. \n\nBut, right, the ticket rotation. I know what I see. No, no, nothing like that. Like I said, it's just the situation at the office. I don't have any, you know, wild theories about aliens or the government or anything. My brother-in-law did that when he was trying to seal around a window frame. He ended up bowing the whole frame so badly the window wouldn't even open. He had to chisel it all out and start over. He's not exactly what you'd call a handyman, though. He's an accountant. Good with numbers, terrible with a hammer. He actually does my taxes every year, which saves me a headache. We took one of those airboat tours, you know, with the huge fan on the back? It was incredibly loud. They give you these cheap little foam earplugs, but they don't do much. We saw a few alligators, mostly just floating there like logs. You'd think they'd be more active, but they're reptiles, so they just bask in the sun to regulate their body temperature. \n\nI used to have a pet iguana when I was in college. His name was Godzilla. Creative, I know. I had to set up this whole heat lamp situation in my dorm room. The RA tried to write me up for it, said it was a fire hazard, but I argued that it was an essential piece of scientific equipment for my biology elective. I wasn't even taking biology, but he didn't know that. \n\nBut anyway, what were we talking about? Oh, right, people plotting against me. No, no, definitely not. It's strictly a problem with management at work. My personal life is perfectly normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed that people were trying to harm you or conspire against you in situations that didn’t involve work?\n\nPatient: Doc, are you testing my memory or something? I feel like we just went over this. Like I literally just said, outside of this issue with the ticket rotation and the audits, everything is completely fine. I still keep my house in order, I pay the bills, I help my wife out. I even handle all the technical parts of my job perfectly—nobody is out there saying my work is sloppy or disorganized. \n\nI actually still pay all our bills by mail, by the way. I know most people do it online now, especially guys in IT, but I like having the paper trail. Though, I will say, the price of stamps lately is getting completely out of hand. Have you seen it? They just raised it again. I remember when a stamp was just a few cents. \n\nMy grandfather actually used to collect them. He had these big, heavy leather binders that smelled like old paper and dust. He'd sit at the kitchen table for hours with these tiny metal tweezers, carefully sliding them behind the little plastic sleeves. He was always hoping to find an Inverted Jenny—that's a famous stamp where they printed the airplane upside down by mistake. It's worth an absolute fortune. He never found one, of course. Mostly he just had regular stamps with birds and old presidents on them. \n\nHe was a very patient man, my grandfather. You need a lot of patience for a hobby like that. I don't think I have the eyes for it anymore, honestly. My prescription changed last year, and I finally had to get bifocals. It took me a solid month to stop tripping over the porch stairs because the bottom part of the lens distorts the floor if you look down too fast. \n\nBut anyway, what I'm saying is, no. No conspiracies or people trying to harm me outside of work. My life outside the office is totally normal.\n\nDoctor: Outside of your concerns about work, have you ever had any unusual beliefs that others don’t share, such as thinking that messages were being sent to you through the TV, radio, or other means?\n\nPatient: Messages through the TV? No, no, I don't think so. I mean, I watch the news, but it's just the news. Mostly I watch those home renovation shows, to be honest. My wife loves them. We were just watching one the other night where this couple bought a house that was completely infested with termites. I mean, the structural damage was incredible. They had to tear out all the load-bearing walls and put in these massive steel I-beams. \n\nI don't know how they afford it, honestly. Those shows always have these ridiculous budgets. The husband will be, like, a part-time butterfly catcher and the wife is a freelance ribbon-tier, and their budget is somehow two million dollars. It makes no sense. But I guess that's television for you. It's all smoke and mirrors. \n\nSpeaking of mirrors, we actually just replaced the one in our master bathroom. The old one was starting to get that black stuff around the edges? Desilvering, they call it. It happens when moisture gets behind the glass and reacts with the silver backing. I had to learn how to hang the new one myself. It's trickier than it looks because you have to use a French cleat system so it sits flush against the drywall. If you just use regular picture wire, it tilts forward and distorts the reflection. \n\nBut, uh, messages? Like secret codes or something? No, nothing like that. Just regular TV.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed that something was happening to you that others didn’t seem to notice or understand, even if it wasn’t related to work?\n\nPatient: Something happening to me that others don't notice? No, I don't think so. I mean, my wife sometimes tells me I don't notice things she does, like when she gets a haircut or moves a piece of furniture. Last week she completely rearranged the living room, moved the sofa to the opposite wall, and I walked right in and sat down to read the paper without even realizing it. She was pretty annoyed about that. \n\nI was reading about this new hybrid car they're coming out with, actually. It's supposed to get something like eighty miles to the gallon. I don't know how they manage that, probably some new battery technology. I've been thinking about trading in my old sedan. It's got over a hundred thousand miles on it, and the transmission is starting to slip a little when it shifts from second to third gear. You can feel this slight hesitation, almost like a little jerk. \n\nI took it to the mechanic, this guy Mike down at the corner garage. He's been working on my cars for fifteen years. He said it's probably the solenoid, but to replace it they'd have to drop the whole transmission pan, and at that point, you might as well just rebuild the whole thing. It's a huge expense. I'm not sure it's worth putting that kind of money into a car that old, you know? \n\nBut anyway, what was the question? Something happening to me? No, no, nothing like that. Just the usual day-to-day stuff. Like I said, the only real issue I'm dealing with is the situation at work. Outside of that, everything is perfectly normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts or beliefs that you know are completely true, but you also realize most other people wouldn’t agree with you about them?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, that's exactly why I'm sitting here talking to you right now. For over a year, I've been trying to get someone—anyone—to take this seriously. The new ticket rotation system and those random security audits they started doing? They are absolutely being used to build a paper trail against me at work. I know it's true. But every time I bring it up to management, they just pull up these system logs and give me the same tired speech about how it's \"just standard policy\" and \"automated distribution.\" \n\nIt's like talking to a brick wall. Have you ever tried to get a straight answer out of corporate HR? It's impossible. They use all these buzzwords, like \"synergy\" and \"optimization.\" My nephew actually works in HR for a big logistics company out in Denver. He's always talking about \"onboarding pipelines\" and \"culture fit.\" \n\nHe just bought a house out there, actually. Beautiful place, right near the mountains. But the altitude really gets to him. He says baking is completely different up there. You have to adjust the flour and the leavening agents because the air pressure is lower, so things rise faster and then collapse. He tried to make a pound cake for his housewarming party, and it came out looking like a hockey puck. \n\nMy wife makes a fantastic pound cake. She uses this old recipe from her grandmother, calls for a whole block of cream cheese in the batter. It makes it incredibly moist. She usually bakes one around the holidays and gives half of it to the neighbors. Our neighbors are nice enough, I suppose. They have a golden retriever that barks at the mailman every single day at exactly 11:30. You could set your watch by it. \n\nBut, right, the ticket rotation. I know what I see. I know how the system is supposed to work, and I know they're manipulating it to target me, even if they won't admit it and everyone else just buys the \"company policy\" line.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly that you knew most people would think was irrational or unfounded, even if it wasn’t related to work?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing like that. Like I said, it's just the situation at the office. I don't have any, you know, wild theories about aliens or the government or anything. The traffic backing up onto the off-ramp is just a nightmare. \n\nI was stuck in it for forty-five minutes yesterday just trying to get to the hardware store. I needed to pick up some of that expanding foam sealant. We've got this draft coming in under the baseboards in the guest bedroom. It's an older house, built in the late seventies, so you expect a little settling, but the gap is getting noticeable. The foam works pretty well, but you have to be careful not to overfill it because it expands like crazy and can actually warp the trim. \n\nMy brother-in-law did that when he was trying to seal around a window frame. He ended up bowing the whole frame so badly the window wouldn't even open. He had to chisel it all out and start over. He's not exactly what you'd call a handyman, though. He's an accountant. Good with numbers, terrible with a hammer. He actually does my taxes every year, which saves me a headache. Tax code is so complicated these days, all those deductions and forms. \n\nBut, uh, no. I don't have any irrational beliefs. Just the facts about what they're doing to me at work. That's the only thing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Strange? No, I don't have any strange beliefs. Everything I'm telling you makes perfect sense, it's just that management refuses to acknowledge it. But outside of the office? I mean, IBM made a solid machine back in the day, built like a tank, but finding someone who actually knows RPG programming now? It's like finding a needle in a haystack. Carl made a killing just because he still had the old manuals in his basement. I used to have a bunch of those old manuals, too, stacked up in my garage. No, no, I... I really don't think so. I mean, I'm here because for over a year now, I've been trying to get someone to take this seriously. Over a year. Even though management keeps pulling me in, showing me the logs, and saying it's just policy... it's not a misunderstanding. \n\nYou know, misunderstandings happen all the time, sure. Like last week at the grocery store, the cashier thought I handed her a twenty, but it was a ten. Simple mistake. The lighting in those places is terrible anyway, all fluorescent. Fluorescent lights actually flicker at a frequency that can cause headaches, did you know that? When I was working at this startup in the early 2000s, they put us in this basement office with these awful flickering tubes. Half the team was popping ibuprofen by noon every day. I ended up bringing in my own desk lamp, just a nice, warm incandescent bulb. Much better for the eyes. You can't even buy those old incandescent bulbs easily anymore, everything is LED. LEDs are efficient, I'll give them that, but the color temperature is always so cold. Makes everything look like a hospital. \n\nBut anyway, no, what's happening at work isn't a coincidence. They keep showing me the logs and saying it's policy, but I know they're just using the new rotation to build that paper trail against me. It's been over a year of this, and I just need someone to finally see what's actually going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever taken any steps to address your concerns, like speaking to HR, filing a formal complaint, or confronting your managers directly?\n\nPatient: Go to HR? No, no, I haven't done anything like that. I haven't filed any formal complaints or confronted my managers. I mean, HR works for management, everyone knows that. If I go to them, it just speeds up whatever they're planning against me. \n\nSpeaking of HR, back in '96 I worked at this telecom place, and the HR director—Susan, I think her name was—she was completely obsessed with team-building exercises. We had to go out to the woods and do these trust falls. Trust falls! Can you believe it? I'm an IT guy, I don't want to fall backwards into the arms of the marketing department. Marketing people always have the weakest grips anyway. They spend all day typing on those little ergonomic keyboards, never lifting anything heavier than a coffee mug. \n\nI had one of those ergonomic keyboards once, actually. The split ones? Microsoft Natural, it was called. Huge white thing, took up half the desk. My cat used to love sleeping right in the middle gap of it where the keys split apart. Barnaby, that was his name. He was a Maine Coon mix, lived to be 18 years old. I fed him nothing but wet food. Dry food is terrible for their kidneys, you know. Most people don't realize cats get almost all their moisture from their diet in the wild. They don't have a strong thirst drive like dogs do. If you feed a cat just dry kibble, you're basically asking for renal failure down the line. The vet bills will bankrupt you. \n\nBut... what was the question? Oh, right, complaining to management. No, I haven't said a word to them directly. I'm just keeping my head down. If I confront them, they'll just deny it anyway.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had your concerns about being targeted at work documented or reviewed by an independent third party, such as a workplace investigator or legal advisor?\n\nPatient: An independent third party? Like a lawyer? No, no, I haven't done that. I haven't had anyone outside look at it. Lawyers are... well, they're expensive for one thing. \n\nMy brother-in-law, Dave, he hired a lawyer a few years back for a property dispute with his neighbor. The neighbor put up this fence, right? Just a standard wooden privacy fence, but he built it about three inches over the property line onto Dave's side. Three inches! You wouldn't think it matters, but Dave is very particular about his lawn. He has one of those riding mowers, a John Deere, bright green. He polishes that thing like it's a sports car. Anyway, the lawyer charged him something like four hundred dollars an hour just to send a letter. Four hundred dollars! I told Dave he could have just gone out there at night with a crowbar and moved the posts himself, but he said that was illegal. \n\nSpeaking of lawns, I used to love mowing the lawn when I was a kid. My dad had this old push mower, the kind with the spinning blades, no motor at all. You had to get a running start to cut through the thick patches. It was a great workout. Kids today, they don't get outside enough. They're all glued to their screens, playing video games. I mean, I work with computers all day, but when I get home, I don't want to look at a monitor. I like to read. Physical books, you know? The smell of paper. You can't get that from an e-reader. \n\nBut, uh, no, I haven't brought in an investigator or anything like that. I don't have that kind of money, and besides, who's going to believe me over a whole corporation?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had similar concerns about being targeted or plotted against in other areas of your life, like with family, friends, or in social situations?\n\nPatient: Targeted or plotted against outside of work? No, no, nothing like that. It's just... it's just this thing at the office with the ticket rotation and the audits. Outside of that, everything is fine. I still keep the house in order, pay the bills on time, help my wife with whatever she needs. I even handle all the technical parts of my job perfectly fine—nobody ever says my work is sloppy or disorganized. \n\nMy wife, actually, she's the one who handles the gardening. She planted these hydrangeas out front last spring. Beautiful blue ones. You know, the color of a hydrangea actually depends on the acidity of the soil? If the soil is acidic, they turn blue, but if it's alkaline, they turn pink. We had to mix all this coffee grounds and pine needles into the dirt to get the pH just right. I used to drink a lot of coffee, black, no sugar. But it started giving me heartburn, so I switched to green tea. \n\nGreen tea is supposed to be full of antioxidants. I read an article that said people in Okinawa drink it all day long and they live to be a hundred. A hundred years old! I don't know if I'd want to live that long, honestly. Think of how much the world would change. When I started in IT, we were still using floppy disks. The big 5-and-a-quarter inch ones that were actually floppy. Now everything is in the cloud. The \"cloud\"—it's just someone else's computer, really. People talk about it like it's magic, but it's just server farms in Virginia drawing enough power to light up a small city. \n\nBut, uh, no, to answer your question, nobody else is plotting against me. My family and friends are fine. It's just the management at work. Outside of that, I'm completely put together.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any situations where you believed people were plotting against you or planning to harm you outside of the workplace, such as in your personal life or in public places?\n\nPatient: Plotting against me outside of work? No, I just said that, didn't I? No, it's just the office. Out in public or in my personal life, I don't have any issues like that. People at the grocery store or on the street aren't paying any attention to me. Everyone is just going about their business. \n\nSpeaking of the grocery store, I went to that new one they opened over on 4th Street last weekend. Have you been there? It's massive. They have a whole aisle just for olive oil. I stood there for ten minutes trying to figure out the difference between extra virgin, cold-pressed, and light tasting. I ended up just buying the one with the nicest bottle. It had a little picture of a Tuscan villa on it. \n\nTuscany is supposed to be beautiful. My wife and I talked about going to Italy for our 25th anniversary, but we ended up going to Florida instead. She wanted to see the Everglades. We took one of those airboat tours, you know, with the huge fan on the back? It was incredibly loud. They give you these cheap little foam earplugs, but they don't do much. We saw a few alligators, mostly just floating there like logs. You'd think they'd be more active, but they're reptiles, so they just bask in the sun to regulate their body temperature. \n\nI used to have a pet iguana when I was in college. His name was Godzilla. Creative, I know. I had to set up this whole heat lamp situation in my dorm room. The RA tried to write me up for it, said it was a fire hazard, but I argued that it was an essential piece of scientific equipment for my biology elective. I wasn't even taking biology, but he didn't know that. \n\nBut anyway, what were we talking about? Oh, right, people plotting against me. No, no, definitely not. It's strictly a problem with management at work. My personal life is perfectly normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed that people were trying to harm you or conspire against you in situations that didn’t involve work?\n\nPatient: Doc, are you testing my memory or something? I feel like we just went over this. Like I literally just said, outside of this issue with the ticket rotation and the audits, everything is completely fine. I still keep my house in order, I pay the bills, I help my wife out. I even handle all the technical parts of my job perfectly—nobody is out there saying my work is sloppy or disorganized. \n\nI actually still pay all our bills by mail, by the way. I know most people do it online now, especially guys in IT, but I like having the paper trail. Though, I will say, the price of stamps lately is getting completely out of hand. Have you seen it? They just raised it again. I remember when a stamp was just a few cents. \n\nMy grandfather actually used to collect them. He had these big, heavy leather binders that smelled like old paper and dust. He'd sit at the kitchen table for hours with these tiny metal tweezers, carefully sliding them behind the little plastic sleeves. He was always hoping to find an Inverted Jenny—that's a famous stamp where they printed the airplane upside down by mistake. It's worth an absolute fortune. He never found one, of course. Mostly he just had regular stamps with birds and old presidents on them. \n\nHe was a very patient man, my grandfather. You need a lot of patience for a hobby like that. I don't think I have the eyes for it anymore, honestly. My prescription changed last year, and I finally had to get bifocals. It took me a solid month to stop tripping over the porch stairs because the bottom part of the lens distorts the floor if you look down too fast. \n\nBut anyway, what I'm saying is, no. No conspiracies or people trying to harm me outside of work. My life outside the office is totally normal.\n\nDoctor: Outside of your concerns about work, have you ever had any unusual beliefs that others don’t share, such as thinking that messages were being sent to you through the TV, radio, or other means?\n\nPatient: Messages through the TV? No, no, I don't think so. I mean, I watch the news, but it's just the news. Mostly I watch those home renovation shows, to be honest. My wife loves them. We were just watching one the other night where this couple bought a house that was completely infested with termites. I mean, the structural damage was incredible. They had to tear out all the load-bearing walls and put in these massive steel I-beams. \n\nI don't know how they afford it, honestly. Those shows always have these ridiculous budgets. The husband will be, like, a part-time butterfly catcher and the wife is a freelance ribbon-tier, and their budget is somehow two million dollars. It makes no sense. But I guess that's television for you. It's all smoke and mirrors. \n\nSpeaking of mirrors, we actually just replaced the one in our master bathroom. The old one was starting to get that black stuff around the edges? Desilvering, they call it. It happens when moisture gets behind the glass and reacts with the silver backing. I had to learn how to hang the new one myself. It's trickier than it looks because you have to use a French cleat system so it sits flush against the drywall. If you just use regular picture wire, it tilts forward and distorts the reflection. \n\nBut, uh, messages? Like secret codes or something? No, nothing like that. Just regular TV.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed that something was happening to you that others didn’t seem to notice or understand, even if it wasn’t related to work?\n\nPatient: Something happening to me that others don't notice? No, I don't think so. I mean, my wife sometimes tells me I don't notice things she does, like when she gets a haircut or moves a piece of furniture. Last week she completely rearranged the living room, moved the sofa to the opposite wall, and I walked right in and sat down to read the paper without even realizing it. She was pretty annoyed about that. \n\nI was reading about this new hybrid car they're coming out with, actually. It's supposed to get something like eighty miles to the gallon. I don't know how they manage that, probably some new battery technology. I've been thinking about trading in my old sedan. It's got over a hundred thousand miles on it, and the transmission is starting to slip a little when it shifts from second to third gear. You can feel this slight hesitation, almost like a little jerk. \n\nI took it to the mechanic, this guy Mike down at the corner garage. He's been working on my cars for fifteen years. He said it's probably the solenoid, but to replace it they'd have to drop the whole transmission pan, and at that point, you might as well just rebuild the whole thing. It's a huge expense. I'm not sure it's worth putting that kind of money into a car that old, you know? \n\nBut anyway, what was the question? Something happening to me? No, no, nothing like that. Just the usual day-to-day stuff. Like I said, the only real issue I'm dealing with is the situation at work. Outside of that, everything is perfectly normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts or beliefs that you know are completely true, but you also realize most other people wouldn’t agree with you about them?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, that's exactly why I'm sitting here talking to you right now. Have you ever tried to get a straight answer out of corporate HR? It's impossible. They use all these buzzwords, like \"synergy\" and \"optimization.\" My nephew actually works in HR for a big logistics company out in Denver. He's always talking about \"onboarding pipelines\" and \"culture fit.\" \n\nHe just bought a house out there, actually. Beautiful place, right near the mountains. But the altitude really gets to him. He says baking is completely different up there. You have to adjust the flour and the leavening agents because the air pressure is lower, so things rise faster and then collapse. He tried to make a pound cake for his housewarming party, and it came out looking like a hockey puck. \n\nMy wife makes a fantastic pound cake. She uses this old recipe from her grandmother, calls for a whole block of cream cheese in the batter. It makes it incredibly moist. She usually bakes one around the holidays and gives half of it to the neighbors. Our neighbors are nice enough, I suppose. They have a golden retriever that barks at the mailman every single day at exactly 11:30. You could set your watch by it. \n\nBut, right, the ticket rotation. I know what I see. I know how the system is supposed to work, and I know they're manipulating it to target me, even if they won't admit it and everyone else just buys the \"company policy\" line.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly that you knew most people would think was irrational or unfounded, even if it wasn’t related to work?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing like that. Like I said, it's just the situation at the office. I don't have any, you know, wild theories about aliens or the government or anything. We were just watching one the other night where this couple bought a house that was completely infested with termites. I mean, the structural damage was incredible. They had to tear out all the load-bearing walls and put in these massive steel I-beams. \n\nI don't know how they afford it, honestly. Those shows always have these ridiculous budgets. The husband will be, like, a part-time butterfly catcher and the wife is a freelance ribbon-tier, and their budget is somehow two million dollars. It makes no sense. But I guess that's television for you. It's all smoke and mirrors. \n\nSpeaking of mirrors, we actually just replaced the one in our master bathroom. The old one was starting to get that black stuff around the edges? Desilvering, they call it. It happens when moisture gets behind the glass and reacts with the silver backing. I had to learn how to hang the new one myself. It's trickier than it looks because you have to use a French cleat system so it sits flush against the drywall. If you just use regular picture wire, it tilts forward and distorts the reflection. \n\nBut, uh, messages? Like secret codes or something? No, nothing like that. Just regular TV.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed that something was happening to you that others didn’t seem to notice or understand, even if it wasn’t related to work?\n\nPatient: Something happening to me that others don't notice? No, I don't think so. I mean, my wife sometimes tells me I don't notice things she does, like when she gets a haircut or moves a piece of furniture. Last week she completely rearranged the living room, moved the sofa to the opposite wall, and I walked right in and sat down to read the paper without even realizing it. She was pretty annoyed about that. \n\nI was reading about this new hybrid car they're coming out with, actually. It's supposed to get something like eighty miles to the gallon. I don't know how they manage that, probably some new battery technology. I've been thinking about trading in my old sedan. It's got over a hundred thousand miles on it, and the transmission is starting to slip a little when it shifts from second to third gear. You can feel this slight hesitation, almost like a little jerk. \n\nI took it to the mechanic, this guy Mike down at the corner garage. He's been working on my cars for fifteen years. He said it's probably the solenoid, but to replace it they'd have to drop the whole transmission pan, and at that point, you might as well just rebuild the whole thing. It's a huge expense. I'm not sure it's worth putting that kind of money into a car that old, you know? \n\nBut anyway, what was the question? Something happening to me? No, no, nothing like that. Just the usual day-to-day stuff. Like I said, the only real issue I'm dealing with is the situation at work. Outside of that, everything is perfectly normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts or beliefs that you know are completely true, but you also realize most other people wouldn’t agree with you about them?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, that's exactly why I'm sitting here talking to you right now. For over a year, I've been trying to get someone—anyone—to take this seriously. The new ticket rotation system and those random security audits they started doing? They are absolutely being used to build a paper trail against me at work. I know it's true. But every time I bring it up to management, they just pull up these system logs and give me the same tired speech about how it's \"just standard policy\" and \"automated distribution.\" \n\nIt's like talking to a brick wall. Have you ever tried to get a straight answer out of corporate HR? It's impossible. They use all these buzzwords, like \"synergy\" and \"optimization.\" My nephew actually works in HR for a big logistics company out in Denver. He's always talking about \"onboarding pipelines\" and \"culture fit.\" \n\nHe just bought a house out there, actually. Beautiful place, right near the mountains. But the altitude really gets to him. He says baking is completely different up there. You have to adjust the flour and the leavening agents because the air pressure is lower, so things rise faster and then collapse. He tried to make a pound cake for his housewarming party, and it came out looking like a hockey puck. \n\nMy wife makes a fantastic pound cake. She uses this old recipe from her grandmother, calls for a whole block of cream cheese in the batter. It makes it incredibly moist. She usually bakes one around the holidays and gives half of it to the neighbors. Our neighbors are nice enough, I suppose. They have a golden retriever that barks at the mailman every single day at exactly 11:30. You could set your watch by it. \n\nBut, right, the ticket rotation. I know what I see. I know how the system is supposed to work, and I know they're manipulating it to target me, even if they won't admit it and everyone else just buys the \"company policy\" line.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly that you knew most people would think was irrational or unfounded, even if it wasn’t related to work?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing like that. Like I said, it's just the situation at the office. I don't have any, you know, wild theories about aliens or the government or anything. I mean, speaking of the government, did you see they're doing construction on the interstate again? They've had that right lane closed for what feels like six months now. The traffic backing up onto the off-ramp is just a nightmare. \n\nI was stuck in it for forty-five minutes yesterday just trying to get to the hardware store. I needed to pick up some of that expanding foam sealant. We've got this draft coming in under the baseboards in the guest bedroom. It's an older house, built in the late seventies, so you expect a little settling, but the gap is getting noticeable. The foam works pretty well, but you have to be careful not to overfill it because it expands like crazy and can actually warp the trim. \n\nMy brother-in-law did that when he was trying to seal around a window frame. He ended up bowing the whole frame so badly the window wouldn't even open. He had to chisel it all out and start over. He's not exactly what you'd call a handyman, though. He's an accountant. Good with numbers, terrible with a hammer. He actually does my taxes every year, which saves me a headache. Tax code is so complicated these days, all those deductions and forms. \n\nBut, uh, no. I don't have any irrational beliefs. Just the facts about what they're doing to me at work. That's the only thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs that you knew were completely true, but you also knew that most people would think they were strange or didn’t make sense?\n\nPatient: Strange? No. Honestly, the only thing that really doesn't make sense to me lately is this new smart thermostat my daughter bought me for my birthday. \n\nShe insisted I needed one that connects to the Wi-Fi. Which is ridiculous because my schedule changes. Like last Tuesday, I had to go to the dentist in the middle of the afternoon for a crown replacement. I cracked a molar on an almond, of all things. Just sitting there eating a handful of mixed nuts watching the evening news, and crack. \n\nDo you know how much a porcelain crown costs these days? It's highway robbery. My dental insurance barely covered a third of it. The dentist, Dr. Aris, he has this fancy new chair in his office that actually massages your back while he works. It's a nice distraction, I guess, but he talks the entire time his hands are in your mouth. You can't answer him. He was going on and on about his golf swing and how he's trying to correct a slice. I don't even play golf. Takes up too much of the weekend, and the greens fees are outrageous. \n\nBut, uh, what were we talking about? Strange beliefs? Yeah, no, nothing like that. No, no, nothing like that outside of the office. Nobody's trying to harm me or... or conspire against me in my personal life. It's strictly the people at work with their system audits. Outside of that, things are perfectly normal. 

Well, I mean, as normal as things can be with the way the neighborhood is changing. Did I mention the new folks who moved in across the street? The Miller house. Old Mr. Miller passed away last spring, bless his heart. He had the most incredible tomato garden. He used to grow these massive beefsteak tomatoes, the size of softballs. He'd bring over a basket every August. My wife would make fresh salsa for weeks. 

But anyway, the new people, they painted the front door this bright, neon yellow. It's... it's really something. I don't know what the homeowner's association is going to say about it. They're usually real sticklers for the approved color palette. Remember when they sent out those warning letters because people were leaving their trash cans out past 6:00 PM on collection day? My neighbor two doors down, the one with the barking golden retriever, he got a fifty-dollar fine. He was furious. Said he got stuck at work and couldn't get home in time. 

He works over at the water treatment plant. Says it's a steady job, good benefits, but the smell... I don't know how he deals with it. I guess you get used to anything after a while. Kind of like how I've had to get used to the constant monitoring at my job. But, yeah, to answer your question, no. Nobody is out to get me outside of work. Just the usual neighborhood drama, you know?

Back in the late nineties, when I was setting up the old Windows NT servers for a different firm, we had a strict policy about network access, and my manager back then—Dave, nice guy, he actually retired down to the Gulf Coast a few years back. Bought himself a little pontoon boat. I always thought about getting a boat myself, but honestly, the fiberglass maintenance on those things, it's just a massive money pit. You leave it in the saltwater too long and it's practically ruined. I actually drove past a boat dealership on the way to your clinic today, right off the highway, and the traffic was just awful around there, backed up for miles because of some construction... \n\nBut anyway, yeah, management keeps pointing at these logs and swearing up and down it's just the new policy, but I know what they're really doing.\n\nDoctor: When you say you know what management is really doing, can you tell me exactly what you believe they are trying to do to you?\n\nPatient: Well, they’re trying to fire me, obviously. Or worse, maybe set me up for some kind of corporate sabotage. Like I said, for over a year now, I’ve been trying to get someone to take this seriously. They implemented this new ticket rotation system, right? And these random security audits. And it is so clear that they are using them to build a paper trail against me. \n\nI mean, I know how these systems work. I’ve been in IT support since before most of these managers were out of high school. Back when you had to actually know how a motherboard worked, not just click a button on a cloud dashboard. Speaking of motherboards, I was trying to fix my nephew's gaming PC last weekend. He bought all these flashy components with RGB lights everywhere. Kids today, they want their computers to look like a disco ball. It’s ridiculous. I told him, \"You don't need all those lights, it just draws more power and creates heat.\" Heat is the enemy of electronics, you know. I had this old server room back in '98, the AC went out in the middle of August... we had box fans blowing everywhere just to keep the hard drives from melting down. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, management keeps pulling me in, showing me these logs, and saying, \"Oh, it’s just the new policy, everyone is getting audited.\" But I know it's a targeted campaign. They're just using the policy as a cover to build a case against me.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me why you believe management specifically targets you and what evidence, if any, supports the idea that this is more than just a policy change aimed at everyone?\n\nPatient: Well, it's... it's obvious to me because of how they look at me when they say it. You know? They get this tight little smile. And the evidence is right there in the ticket queue. I'll get assigned the most impossible, convoluted legacy system issues right before the end of my shift. It's designed to make me miss the SLA metrics. \n\nSpeaking of legacy systems, you wouldn't believe what some of these companies are still running. I had a buddy, Carl, who did consulting for a local bank a few years ago. They were still using an AS/400 system for their core processing. AS/400! Can you imagine? I mean, IBM made a solid machine back in the day, built like a tank, but finding someone who actually knows RPG programming now? It's like finding a needle in a haystack. Carl made a killing just because he still had the old manuals in his basement. I used to have a bunch of those old manuals, too, stacked up in my garage. But my wife, my ex-wife I should say, she made me throw them out when we moved to the new house in 2010. Said they smelled like mildew. She was always complaining about the smell of things. Even the dog, she said the dog always smelled like corn chips, which, you know, is just what dog paws smell like. It's a bacteria thing, completely natural. \n\nBut anyway... what was I... right, the evidence. It's just, I know they're not doing this to the younger guys. The guys who just started out of college, they get the easy password resets and software installs. But me? It's always the impossible tasks, right when the audit is scheduled.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered that these events might just be coincidences or misunderstandings, and not a deliberate effort to target you?\n\nPatient: Coincidences? No, no, I... I really don't think so. I mean, I'm here because for over a year now, I've been trying to get someone to take this seriously. Over a year. The new ticket rotation, the security audits... it's all being used to build a paper trail against me. I know it is. Even though management keeps pulling me in, showing me the logs, and saying it's just policy... it's not a misunderstanding. \n\nYou know, misunderstandings happen all the time, sure. Like last week at the grocery store, the cashier thought I handed her a twenty, but it was a ten. Simple mistake. The lighting in those places is terrible anyway, all fluorescent. Fluorescent lights actually flicker at a frequency that can cause headaches, did you know that? When I was working at this startup in the early 2000s, they put us in this basement office with these awful flickering tubes. Half the team was popping ibuprofen by noon every day. I ended up bringing in my own desk lamp, just a nice, warm incandescent bulb. Much better for the eyes. You can't even buy those old incandescent bulbs easily anymore, everything is LED. LEDs are efficient, I'll give them that, but the color temperature is always so cold. Makes everything look like a hospital. \n\nBut anyway, no, what's happening at work isn't a coincidence. They keep showing me the logs and saying it's policy, but I know they're just using the new rotation to build that paper trail against me. It's been over a year of this, and I just need someone to finally see what's actually going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever taken any steps to address your concerns, like speaking to HR, filing a formal complaint, or confronting your managers directly?\n\nPatient: Go to HR? No, no, I haven't done anything like that. I haven't filed any formal complaints or confronted my managers. I mean, HR works for management, everyone knows that. If I go to them, it just speeds up whatever they're planning against me. \n\nSpeaking of HR, back in '96 I worked at this telecom place, and the HR director—Susan, I think her name was—she was completely obsessed with team-building exercises. We had to go out to the woods and do these trust falls. Trust falls! Can you believe it? I'm an IT guy, I don't want to fall backwards into the arms of the marketing department. Marketing people always have the weakest grips anyway. They spend all day typing on those little ergonomic keyboards, never lifting anything heavier than a coffee mug. \n\nI had one of those ergonomic keyboards once, actually. The split ones? Microsoft Natural, it was called. Huge white thing, took up half the desk. My cat used to love sleeping right in the middle gap of it where the keys split apart. Barnaby, that was his name. He was a Maine Coon mix, lived to be 18 years old. I fed him nothing but wet food. Dry food is terrible for their kidneys, you know. Most people don't realize cats get almost all their moisture from their diet in the wild. They don't have a strong thirst drive like dogs do. If you feed a cat just dry kibble, you're basically asking for renal failure down the line. The vet bills will bankrupt you. \n\nBut... what was the question? Oh, right, complaining to management. No, I haven't said a word to them directly. I'm just keeping my head down. If I confront them, they'll just deny it anyway.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had your concerns about being targeted at work documented or reviewed by an independent third party, such as a workplace investigator or legal advisor?\n\nPatient: An independent third party? Like a lawyer? No, no, I haven't done that. I haven't had anyone outside look at it. Just a standard wooden privacy fence, but he built it about three inches over the property line onto Dave's side. Three inches! I read an article that said people in Okinawa drink it all day long and they live to be a hundred. A hundred years old! I don't know if I'd want to live that long, honestly. When I was working at this startup in the early 2000s, they put us in this basement office with these awful flickering tubes. Half the team was popping ibuprofen by noon every day. I ended up bringing in my own desk lamp, just a nice, warm incandescent bulb. Much better for the eyes. You can't even buy those old incandescent bulbs easily anymore, everything is LED. LEDs are efficient, I'll give them that, but the color temperature is always so cold. Makes everything look like a hospital. \n\nBut anyway, no, what's happening at work isn't a coincidence. They keep showing me the logs and saying it's policy, but I know they're just using the new rotation to build that paper trail against me. It's been over a year of this, and I just need someone to finally see what's actually going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever taken any steps to address your concerns, like speaking to HR, filing a formal complaint, or confronting your managers directly?\n\nPatient: Go to HR? No, no, I haven't done anything like that. I haven't filed any formal complaints or confronted my managers. I mean, HR works for management, everyone knows that. If I go to them, it just speeds up whatever they're planning against me. \n\nSpeaking of HR, back in '96 I worked at this telecom place, and the HR director—Susan, I think her name was—she was completely obsessed with team-building exercises. We had to go out to the woods and do these trust falls. Trust falls! Can you believe it? I'm an IT guy, I don't want to fall backwards into the arms of the marketing department. Marketing people always have the weakest grips anyway. They spend all day typing on those little ergonomic keyboards, never lifting anything heavier than a coffee mug. \n\nI had one of those ergonomic keyboards once, actually. The split ones? Microsoft Natural, it was called. Huge white thing, took up half the desk. My cat used to love sleeping right in the middle gap of it where the keys split apart. Barnaby, that was his name. He was a Maine Coon mix, lived to be 18 years old. I fed him nothing but wet food. Dry food is terrible for their kidneys, you know. Most people don't realize cats get almost all their moisture from their diet in the wild. They don't have a strong thirst drive like dogs do. If you feed a cat just dry kibble, you're basically asking for renal failure down the line. The vet bills will bankrupt you. \n\nBut... what was the question? Oh, right, complaining to management. No, I haven't said a word to them directly. I'm just keeping my head down. If I confront them, they'll just deny it anyway.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had your concerns about being targeted at work documented or reviewed by an independent third party, such as a workplace investigator or legal advisor?\n\nPatient: An independent third party? Like a lawyer? No, no, I haven't done that. I haven't had anyone outside look at it. Lawyers are... well, they're expensive for one thing. \n\nMy brother-in-law, Dave, he hired a lawyer a few years back for a property dispute with his neighbor. The neighbor put up this fence, right? Just a standard wooden privacy fence, but he built it about three inches over the property line onto Dave's side. Three inches! You wouldn't think it matters, but Dave is very particular about his lawn. He has one of those riding mowers, a John Deere, bright green. He polishes that thing like it's a sports car. Anyway, the lawyer charged him something like four hundred dollars an hour just to send a letter. Four hundred dollars! I told Dave he could have just gone out there at night with a crowbar and moved the posts himself, but he said that was illegal. \n\nSpeaking of lawns, I used to love mowing the lawn when I was a kid. My dad had this old push mower, the kind with the spinning blades, no motor at all. You had to get a running start to cut through the thick patches. It was a great workout. Kids today, they don't get outside enough. They're all glued to their screens, playing video games. I mean, I work with computers all day, but when I get home, I don't want to look at a monitor. I like to read. Physical books, you know? The smell of paper. You can't get that from an e-reader. \n\nBut, uh, no, I haven't brought in an investigator or anything like that. I don't have that kind of money, and besides, who's going to believe me over a whole corporation?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had similar concerns about being targeted or plotted against in other areas of your life, like with family, friends, or in social situations?\n\nPatient: Targeted or plotted against outside of work? No, no, nothing like that. It's just... it's just this thing at the office with the ticket rotation and the audits. Outside of that, everything is fine. I still keep the house in order, pay the bills on time, help my wife with whatever she needs. I even handle all the technical parts of my job perfectly fine—nobody ever says my work is sloppy or disorganized. \n\nMy wife, actually, she's the one who handles the gardening. She planted these hydrangeas out front last spring. Beautiful blue ones. You know, the color of a hydrangea actually depends on the acidity of the soil? If the soil is acidic, they turn blue, but if it's alkaline, they turn pink. We had to mix all this coffee grounds and pine needles into the dirt to get the pH just right. I used to drink a lot of coffee, black, no sugar. But it started giving me heartburn, so I switched to green tea. \n\nGreen tea is supposed to be full of antioxidants. I read an article that said people in Okinawa drink it all day long and they live to be a hundred. A hundred years old! I don't know if I'd want to live that long, honestly. Think of how much the world would change. When I started in IT, we were still using floppy disks. The big 5-and-a-quarter inch ones that were actually floppy. Now everything is in the cloud. The \"cloud\"—it's just someone else's computer, really. People talk about it like it's magic, but it's just server farms in Virginia drawing enough power to light up a small city. \n\nBut, uh, no, to answer your question, nobody else is plotting against me. My family and friends are fine. It's just the management at work. Outside of that, I'm completely put together.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any situations where you believed people were plotting against you or planning to harm you outside of the workplace, such as in your personal life or in public places?\n\nPatient: Plotting against me outside of work? No, I just said that, didn't I? No, it's just the office. Out in public or in my personal life, I don't have any issues like that. People at the grocery store or on the street aren't paying any attention to me. Everyone is just going about their business. \n\nSpeaking of the grocery store, I went to that new one they opened over on 4th Street last weekend. Have you been there? It's massive. They have a whole aisle just for olive oil. I stood there for ten minutes trying to figure out the difference between extra virgin, cold-pressed, and light tasting. I ended up just buying the one with the nicest bottle. It had a little picture of a Tuscan villa on it. \n\nTuscany is supposed to be beautiful. My wife and I talked about going to Italy for our 25th anniversary, but we ended up going to Florida instead. She wanted to see the Everglades. We took one of those airboat tours, you know, with the huge fan on the back? It was incredibly loud. They give you these cheap little foam earplugs, but they don't do much. We saw a few alligators, mostly just floating there like logs. You'd think they'd be more active, but they're reptiles, so they just bask in the sun to regulate their body temperature. \n\nI used to have a pet iguana when I was in college. His name was Godzilla. Creative, I know. I had to set up this whole heat lamp situation in my dorm room. The RA tried to write me up for it, said it was a fire hazard, but I argued that it was an essential piece of scientific equipment for my biology elective. I wasn't even taking biology, but he didn't know that. \n\nBut anyway, what were we talking about? Oh, right, people plotting against me. No, no, definitely not. It's strictly a problem with management at work. My personal life is perfectly normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed that people were trying to harm you or conspire against you in situations that didn’t involve work?\n\nPatient: Doc, are you testing my memory or something? I feel like we just went over this. Like I literally just said, outside of this issue with the ticket rotation and the audits, everything is completely fine. I still keep my house in order, I pay the bills, I help my wife out. I even handle all the technical parts of my job perfectly—nobody is out there saying my work is sloppy or disorganized. \n\nI actually still pay all our bills by mail, by the way. I know most people do it online now, especially guys in IT, but I like having the paper trail. Though, I will say, the price of stamps lately is getting completely out of hand. Have you seen it? They just raised it again. I remember when a stamp was just a few cents. \n\nMy grandfather actually used to collect them. He had these big, heavy leather binders that smelled like old paper and dust. He'd sit at the kitchen table for hours with these tiny metal tweezers, carefully sliding them behind the little plastic sleeves. He was always hoping to find an Inverted Jenny—that's a famous stamp where they printed the airplane upside down by mistake. It's worth an absolute fortune. He never found one, of course. Mostly he just had regular stamps with birds and old presidents on them. \n\nHe was a very patient man, my grandfather. You need a lot of patience for a hobby like that. I don't think I have the eyes for it anymore, honestly. My prescription changed last year, and I finally had to get bifocals. It took me a solid month to stop tripping over the porch stairs because the bottom part of the lens distorts the floor if you look down too fast. \n\nBut anyway, what I'm saying is, no. No conspiracies or people trying to harm me outside of work. My life outside the office is totally normal.\n\nDoctor: Outside of your concerns about work, have you ever had any unusual beliefs that others don’t share, such as thinking that messages were being sent to you through the TV, radio, or other means?\n\nPatient: Messages through the TV? No, no, I don't think so. I mean, I watch the news, but it's just the news. Mostly I watch those home renovation shows, to be honest. My wife loves them. We were just watching one the other night where this couple bought a house that was completely infested with termites. I mean, the structural damage was incredible. They had to tear out all the load-bearing walls and put in these massive steel I-beams. \n\nI don't know how they afford it, honestly. Those shows always have these ridiculous budgets. The husband will be, like, a part-time butterfly catcher and the wife is a freelance ribbon-tier, and their budget is somehow two million dollars. It makes no sense. But I guess that's television for you. It's all smoke and mirrors. \n\nSpeaking of mirrors, we actually just replaced the one in our master bathroom. The old one was starting to get that black stuff around the edges? Desilvering, they call it. It happens when moisture gets behind the glass and reacts with the silver backing. I had to learn how to hang the new one myself. It's trickier than it looks because you have to use a French cleat system so it sits flush against the drywall. If you just use regular picture wire, it tilts forward and distorts the reflection. \n\nBut, uh, messages? Like secret codes or something? No, nothing like that. Just regular TV.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed that something was happening to you that others didn’t seem to notice or understand, even if it wasn’t related to work?\n\nPatient: Something happening to me that others don't notice? No, I don't think so. I mean, my wife sometimes tells me I don't notice things she does, like when she gets a haircut or moves a piece of furniture. Last week she completely rearranged the living room, moved the sofa to the opposite wall, and I walked right in and sat down to read the paper without even realizing it. She was pretty annoyed about that. \n\nI was reading about this new hybrid car they're coming out with, actually. It's supposed to get something like eighty miles to the gallon. I don't know how they manage that, probably some new battery technology. I've been thinking about trading in my old sedan. It's got over a hundred thousand miles on it, and the transmission is starting to slip a little when it shifts from second to third gear. You can feel this slight hesitation, almost like a little jerk. \n\nI took it to the mechanic, this guy Mike down at the corner garage. He's been working on my cars for fifteen years. He said it's probably the solenoid, but to replace it they'd have to drop the whole transmission pan, and at that point, you might as well just rebuild the whole thing. It's a huge expense. I'm not sure it's worth putting that kind of money into a car that old, you know? \n\nBut anyway, what was the question? Something happening to me? No, no, nothing like that. Just the usual day-to-day stuff. Like I said, the only real issue I'm dealing with is the situation at work. Outside of that, everything is perfectly normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts or beliefs that you know are completely true, but you also realize most other people wouldn’t agree with you about them?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, that's exactly why I'm sitting here talking to you right now. For over a year, I've been trying to get someone—anyone—to take this seriously. The new ticket rotation system and those random security audits they started doing? They are absolutely being used to build a paper trail against me at work. I know it's true. But every time I bring it up to management, they just pull up these system logs and give me the same tired speech about how it's \"just standard policy\" and \"automated distribution.\" \n\nIt's like talking to a brick wall. Have you ever tried to get a straight answer out of corporate HR? It's impossible. They use all these buzzwords, like \"synergy\" and \"optimization.\" My nephew actually works in HR for a big logistics company out in Denver. He's always talking about \"onboarding pipelines\" and \"culture fit.\" \n\nHe just bought a house out there, actually. Beautiful place, right near the mountains. But the altitude really gets to him. He says baking is completely different up there. You have to adjust the flour and the leavening agents because the air pressure is lower, so things rise faster and then collapse. He tried to make a pound cake for his housewarming party, and it came out looking like a hockey puck. \n\nMy wife makes a fantastic pound cake. She uses this old recipe from her grandmother, calls for a whole block of cream cheese in the batter. It makes it incredibly moist. She usually bakes one around the holidays and gives half of it to the neighbors. Our neighbors are nice enough, I suppose. They have a golden retriever that barks at the mailman every single day at exactly 11:30. You could set your watch by it. \n\nBut, right, the ticket rotation. I know what I see. I know how the system is supposed to work, and I know they're manipulating it to target me, even if they won't admit it and everyone else just buys the \"company policy\" line.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly that you knew most people would think was irrational or unfounded, even if it wasn’t related to work?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing like that. Like I said, it's just the situation at the office. I don't have any, you know, wild theories about aliens or the government or anything. I mean, speaking of the government, did you see they're doing construction on the interstate again? They've had that right lane closed for what feels like six months now. The traffic backing up onto the off-ramp is just a nightmare. \n\nI was stuck in it for forty-five minutes yesterday just trying to get to the hardware store. I needed to pick up some of that expanding foam sealant. We've got this draft coming in under the baseboards in the guest bedroom. It's an older house, built in the late seventies, so you expect a little settling, but the gap is getting noticeable. The foam works pretty well, but you have to be careful not to overfill it because it expands like crazy and can actually warp the trim. \n\nMy brother-in-law did that when he was trying to seal around a window frame. He ended up bowing the whole frame so badly the window wouldn't even open. He had to chisel it all out and start over. He's not exactly what you'd call a handyman, though. He's an accountant. Good with numbers, terrible with a hammer. He actually does my taxes every year, which saves me a headache. Tax code is so complicated these days, all those deductions and forms. \n\nBut, uh, no. I don't have any irrational beliefs. Just the facts about what they're doing to me at work. That's the only thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs that you knew were completely true, but you also knew that most people would think they were strange or didn’t make sense?\n\nPatient: Strange? No, I don't have any strange beliefs. Everything I'm telling you makes perfect sense, it's just that management refuses to acknowledge it. But outside of the office? No. Honestly, the only thing that really doesn't make sense to me lately is this new smart thermostat my daughter bought me for my birthday. \n\nShe insisted I needed one that connects to the Wi-Fi. Why does a thermostat need Wi-Fi? It's supposed to just read the temperature in the hallway and turn the furnace on. But now it tries to learn my schedule. Which is ridiculous because my schedule changes. Like last Tuesday, I had to go to the dentist in the middle of the afternoon for a crown replacement. I cracked a molar on an almond, of all things. Just sitting there eating a handful of mixed nuts watching the evening news, and crack. \n\nDo you know how much a porcelain crown costs these days? It's highway robbery. My dental insurance barely covered a third of it. The dentist, Dr. Aris, he has this fancy new chair in his office that actually massages your back while he works. It's a nice distraction, I guess, but he talks the entire time his hands are in your mouth. You can't answer him. He was going on and on about his golf swing and how he's trying to correct a slice. I don't even play golf. Takes up too much of the weekend, and the greens fees are outrageous. \n\nBut, uh, what were we talking about? Strange beliefs? Yeah, no, nothing like that. Just the reality of the ticket system at work. That's it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed that people were trying to harm you or conspire against you in situations that didn't involve work?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing like that outside of the office. Nobody's trying to harm me or... or conspire against me in my personal life. It's strictly the people at work with their system audits. Outside of that, things are perfectly normal. \n\nWell, I mean, as normal as things can be with the way the neighborhood is changing. Did I mention the new folks who moved in across the street? The Miller house. Old Mr. Miller passed away last spring, bless his heart. He had the most incredible tomato garden. He used to grow these massive beefsteak tomatoes, the size of softballs. He'd bring over a basket every August. My wife would make fresh salsa for weeks. \n\nBut anyway, the new people, they painted the front door this bright, neon yellow. It's... it's really something. I don't know what the homeowner's association is going to say about it. They're usually real sticklers for the approved color palette. Remember when they sent out those warning letters because people were leaving their trash cans out past 6:00 PM on collection day? My neighbor two doors down, the one with the barking golden retriever, he got a fifty-dollar fine. He was furious. Said he got stuck at work and couldn't get home in time. \n\nHe works over at the water treatment plant. Says it's a steady job, good benefits, but the smell... I don't know how he deals with it. I guess you get used to anything after a while. Kind of like how I've had to get used to the constant monitoring at my job. But, yeah, to answer your question, no. Nobody is out to get me outside of work. Just the usual neighborhood drama, you know?"
